on behalf of the id group. madam president, by definition, the respect we owe to nature, the very thing that provides us with our living space, requires us not to waste anything of what it gives us. the new circular economy action plan is part of this. as such, it would be a good idea to accept, with some reservations, that 50% of greenhouse gas emissions and 90% of biodiversity loss are the result of extracting and processing the planet's resources. this particularly important issue has two aspects. the first, which is perfectly acceptable, envisages the almost endless recycling of our mineral and particularly energy resources, our plant resources which are nutrients and regulators of co2, and even our animal resources, with due respect for animals. it is an immediate winner if we all participate in the reversal of 50 years of blind and unbridled consumption. in the medium term, this is a great opportunity to reorient and re-establish our industry at the end of the model to create more local sectors and many jobs. but there is a more worrying phase, the already binding phase of the bucolic green deal and the paris accords driven by militant environmentalism. climate change is clearly being exploited by the proponents of the world energy power. the european union is setting standards, which risk depriving the member states of any industrial autonomy. just as yesterday, the chinese, with their low-cost industrial power, have imposed a low-cost market in europe. because a recycled product is cheaper to produce and sell, consumers are persuaded to keep their wages as low and precarious as possible, while retaining purchasing power. this is a real danger. it is a major part of our future that is at stake in this matter, which we will vote on while denouncing potential deviations.
the verts/ale group is therefore in favour of the proposal. i would like to thank the president-in-office. first of all, i would like to thank mr huitema and the other rapporteurs for a really strong report. in particular, there is a strong focus on binding reduction targets. europe's overconsumption is enormous. we're using resources as if we had almost three planets. we must reduce our consumption by two thirds! it is also important that the report stresses that the circular economy must be non-toxic and sustainable. if the circles are filled with poison, or if the consumption is too great for the planet to keep up, then it's not going to work. in january, the european environment agency concluded in a report that increased efficiency and new technologies are not enough to make our economy sustainable. the environment agency also shows that sustainable growth, as we like to call it, is hardly possible. in other words, we must free ourselves from dependence on endless economic growth and find an economy in which we can have well-being with prosperity and solidarity within the limits of what the earth can handle. we must now begin the difficult debate. the epa's word that the biodiversity, climate and pollution crises are closely linked to economic growth, we can't just ignore. but at the same time we must avoid being trapped in unemployment and other misery. we must not allow society to go dormant. what we need now are analyses, models and concrete examples of how people and societies can thrive in a true circular economy, where the limits of nature are respected.

in the name of the id group. madam president, colleagues, vice president dombrovskis, exactly one year after the start of the covid-19 pandemic, we are finally voting on the instrument that should strongly support our economies. however, i regret to point out that the conditionalities included, if not applied in a sufficiently flexible manner, could create problems for member states that decide to activate the recovery and resilience facility in both grant and loan form. the underlying problem of the rrf regulation is that it incorporates the compliance with the recommendations of the country report of the years 2019-2020, therefore prior to covid-19, and this crystallizes the criteria for calculating the output gap contained in the cam (commonly agreed methodology), which, on the other hand, is the subject of criticism by many member countries. following this pattern, the suspended stability and growth pact would reappear in full procyclical force. on the basis of a reasonable modification of the cam methodology, it is therefore possible to leave more room for manoeuvre to the member states without the current serious risk of generating hysteresis in excess unemployment and of amplifying the fall in domestic demand. it is equally important to point out the constraints on growth due to the next generation eu agenda which, due to the type of planned investments not adjusted to the real needs posed by the pandemic, will not promote the expected growth but strong disputes with the commission and among the member countries about the level of fiscal consolidation inevitably corresponding to the due financing of the interventions rigidly schematized in the new generation. the crisis generated by the pandemic can therefore be a great opportunity to finally is prohibited by the treaties. the same cannot be said of the ecb's de facto perpetuation of the renewals of those government bonds purchased under the pepp programme, of course always using the capital key criterion. it would be a question of freezing sine die good debt - to use mario draghi's definition of debt contracted to support growth. it is a question of political will alone, not of madam president, this recovery mechanism has three main problems which i would summarize as follows: how much, who pays and under what conditions will it be applied? first of all , let 's not make this into something it 's not: the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. it's not even enough to mitigate the impacts of the pandemic let alone to revive and modernize economies. it's not a silver bullet, it's not really a bazooka. secondly, what is most likely, what is certain at the moment, is that this mechanism will be reimbursed by the member states themselves who now receive it on account of future payments. they will pay what they receive now with cuts in future budget transfers. thirdly, this mechanism is tied to the economic and political conditionality imposed by the european union. to understand what this means, i would remind you that two years ago, in the european commission's country recommendations, the european commission called for portugal to cut health expenditure. there is always a margin of political manoeuvre in the application of this as of any other regulation. but to leave the application of this money to the dictates of these gentlemen cannot be a good thing.

madam president, the recovery and resilience facility (rrf) is a crucial part of the eu’s response to the economic shock caused by the pandemic. it is already a historic achievement in at least three aspects: first, the amount of money it will mobilise; second, the way it will be financed through common debt; and third, the priorities it will have to concentrate on, mainly relating to the climate and the digital transition, but also social cohesion and resilience. however, we need to make these funds a success also in another aspect: in their implementation and execution. and for this it is imperative that projects financed by the facility address structural deficits and bring about real transformation of our economies, that in the private sector smes can also become the beneficiaries of these funds and sub-state governments, having first-hand information on the needs of their economies and territories, get involved in the whole cycle of decision-making and governance of the investment plans. the recovery and transformation of some of our economies may largely rely on the smooth execution of these funds.

mr president, the recovery and resilience facility is not only a way to address the effects of the covid crisis, but a great leap forward towards a closer and deeper union. it complements the action of the european central bank and partially overcomes the lack of a common european fiscal capacity for the time being. however, we would be wrong to consider this facility as the final destination of our journey. this is just the beginning. full recovery will require time, energy, commitment and a great sense of responsibility. in order to succeed, new forms of dialogue and interaction, solidarity and mutual understanding are required. now, more than ever, it’s clear that having a solely national perspective for economic and budgetary measures is not only pretentious, but is short-sighted and inefficient. so a truly european approach is the only way to foster territorial cohesion, to fight social inequalities, and to support policies dedicated to the next generation of europeans. as a parliament, over the next years, we will be vigilant to ensure that the commission carries out the tasks assigned in appropriate ways so that the facility reaches its objectives, especially in terms of digital and environmental transformation, social impact and the next generation policies that we have envisaged for the union. it will not be easy. there will be bumps along the road and divisive forces will always be around the corner, and we got a sample from the debate this morning. so i say to my colleagues that we have created a powerful instrument for unity and solidarity. now it’s our responsibility to make sure that what was conceived to unite us will not end up dividing us.

madam president, ladies and gentlemen, we have welcomed the eu strategy on vaccines and the centralisation of negotiations with the pharmaceutical companies. today we must say with equal clarity that this process has not worked as it should and that much is still unclear as regards the contents of the negotiated contracts and the failed supply plans. in this connection, it seems to me peculiar that only on 4 december last the commission asked commissioner breton to lead a new working party with the aim of identifying and removing bottlenecks in the vaccine production chain. it is likely that vaccination will need to be repeated in the coming years to deal with new variants. we therefore urgently need a clear report on what has not worked, an emergency plan to speed up the vaccination process this year and an updated, credible, long-term vaccine strategy that takes account of production and contract issues.
in the name of the id group. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the big platforms and social media undoubtedly offer great opportunities, which are useful to society, especially in today's world where physical distances have increased, but they must operate within clear rules. the web giants have been claiming for years that they are neutral containers. today, however, it is clear that their policies have real-world implications, as they proclaim themselves as the censors of what messages are acceptable and what are not. and this brings us to a big question, which has precise political and legal implications: who gets to decide what can be posted in the digital space? you see, i don't think there's any vested interest here. it is not a question of deciding whether to defend the ideas of a party or an individual elected official, but of protecting the values of democracy and freedom of expression, including that of individual citizens. that is why we as the league have asked for this debate. i believe that a possible blackout of donald trump or ursula von der leyen or a newspaper or anyone should be decided by laws that define clear parameters and procedures, and not by guidelines made in-house by private giants who act as quasi-judges of democracy. this is a role for the legislators, which we cannot and must not abdicate. the european union must act without delay to safeguard the democratic and free debate on social issues.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, in the media, diversity and pluralism should be linked by the same fundamental objective in a liberal democracy: the aim of the project is to provide citizens with a variety of content. we know that in the dsa, especially the large platforms are in fact obliged to have internal mechanisms to receive notifications from any person or entity of content deemed illegal. the same article provides that the notifications received give rise to actual knowledge or awareness, effectively requiring the removal of allegedly illegal content or disabling access to it. in essence, any report made in supposed good faith requires removal. i think it is unwise that the subjective assessment of individual persons or bodies can have a decisive influence on the full respect of freedom of speech. do we want to go in the opposite direction to the pluralism which should be the hallmark of a democracy? i think that a deep reflection is urgent.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, we as the ecr have asked for this debate because the majority of our social, educational, economic and political relations are now conducted on digital platforms, and it is not possible to deny the regulation of private multinationals, especially if this affects our democracies. i would like to ask the commissioner whether he is aware that the commission has not yet taken a decision on the matter, and whether it is not in the interests of the community to take a decision on the matter. what is illegal offline must also be illegal online, it was said a little while ago. right, right. this is the so-called reserve of law which is the basis of the rule of law.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, in recent years citizens all over the world have been giving voice to their thoughts and reflections through social media, fantastic vehicles of freedom, often censored and made inaccessible by the world's freedom-threatening dictatorships. but lately, there's been a very worrying trend from these multinational communications companies. millions of citizens are censored every day by officials who sit behind a desk and decide what is right or wrong to think. is it right that de facto monopoly companies should have this enormous power? what is a free man but his word and his thought? who is it that has the right to judge what is right or wrong? the judges in the courts or the facebook and twitter gentlemen who invented successful apps? the most disturbing element is that digital oligopolies are appropriating a real public function, that of deciding what is tolerable or not in the debate. in the rule of law, it is the law that decides what is right or wrong, what is punishable or censurable. these multinationals not only consider themselves above the law, but also above european and national taxation. small business owners and vat groups are currently being hammered by the crisis and taxation, and perhaps even censored for their social media views. free citizens who want to be able to express themselves demand to be able to exercise this inviolable right freely, including on the internet. transparency and freedom are two european values first and foremost: let's show that, even through proper digital democracy.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, 25 years after the fourth un world conference on women in beijing, and despite numerous cases of formal recognition and progress on gender equality, women and men do not enjoy the same rights and social, economic and cultural inequalities persist. today, poverty, education, health and violence still pose enormous challenges to women all over the world and to the achievement of an equal society. there is a strong need to address the stereotypes that continue to be the main obstacle to the enhancement of women's skills and the cultural root of violence. the key words for change are: awareness and education as a transformative lever to bring about change that can no longer be delayed.
madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the good text is the result of a debate which has focused on many of the issues we have raised. i am pleased and thank the rapporteur for this important achievement. our priority has always been to achieve a real level playing field in the internal market by removing the obstacles which in the past have prevented the free movement of goods within the union by creating unjustified and arbitrary barriers. these obstacles resulting from unilateral changes have caused damage to italian companies. the elimination of the barriers to free movement which have arisen over time is the direction in which we wanted to go if we are to have a union which is really at the service of business.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, with a simple card, disabled people could have access to the same services in all 27 member states and should not be subjected to any further discrimination. to date, no disabled person has been awarded a european qualification. in italy, only the machine mark gives them recognition, with enormous difficulties in accessing all other services. the debate on this subject has been going on for too long, which is why i have also tabled a question to speed up the creation of a european disability card. we don't make life more difficult for people who already have huge problems. this card could have benefits in the world of work, where the employment data is alarming: 50% of the disabled are unemployed. a simple card could give them a huge advantage. hence my commitment to work together to achieve this goal.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, in an ideal world, work is synonymous with emancipation and inclusion, but in the europe of austerity before and of the pandemic after, for many people with disabilities, work is a mirage. it is unacceptable that ten years after the un convention on the rights of persons with disabilities, nothing has changed in so many member states. this resolution is a wake-up call as the commission launches the new disability strategy. i believe that europe cannot be satisfied with statements of principle, but must provide for concrete policies, such as the portability of rights, the right to independence, full access to work and to education and essential services. we therefore call for social objectives to be included in the european semester, addressing the issue of multiple discrimination and providing for mandatory quotas for recruitment in public institutions, as in the private sector, with appropriate sanctions for non-compliance. people with disabilities are demanding to work for their own fulfillment and to finally receive what has been denied them in terms of opportunities and quality of life.
madam president, commissioner, i have to ask you to stop. in the eu institutions, i often hear the slogan 'so no one is left behind'. we have to be aware that people with disabilities are being left behind and unfortunately the slogan is still just a slogan. in the european union as a whole, only slightly more than 50% of people with disabilities are in employment. this is a group at risk of extreme poverty and social exclusion. inequality in access to the labour market is therefore a fact and we must continue to work to ensure that people with disabilities are active in employment and are self-employed. however, this requires multi-dimensional actions targeting both future and current disabled workers, employers and the general public. people with disabilities are capable and reliable workers. however, if we want to ensure equal access to the workplace or to the education and training system, we must bear in mind that this often requires specific solutions. so i'm all for creating all kinds of incentives, including tax incentives, for the entrepreneurs who employ these people. the removal of physical barriers in the workplace and the creation of an accessibility climate, as well as the promotion of flexible solutions and the full use of the potential of new technologies, are also important.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, in the last year the cost of the pandemic has unfortunately been borne even more by the already disadvantaged groups, such as the disabled. the latest figures clearly show us intolerable employment discrimination within the member states. we must therefore commit ourselves to the full implementation of the convention on the rights of persons with disabilities. it is a subject which is very close to my heart and i had the honour of representing this very parliament at the conference of the contracting parties at the united nations in new york a few years ago. we need to increase social protection, introducing employment quotas for disabled people, guaranteeing equal pay, increasing investment in training and accessible mobility - i am thinking of the great work done on this last issue by activists such as iacopo melio, now regional councillor and european citizen of the year, and my fellow citizen nicola torre - because the participation of people with disabilities is needed in the implementation of policies that affect them. there's a lot to do. reform the european accessibility act and support the strategy of the european commission. we're ready to work. the rights can't just be on paper.
member of the commission. – madam president, on behalf of the commission, i welcome your report on the implementation of this report, and i really thank you for this wide-ranging debate and very important discussion, and i am grateful for your valuable opinions and recommendations. we agree on many of them, and i believe that we are able to take a lot of them into account in the preparation of the new disability strategy. so the implementation is ahead of us. we intend to work closely with member states, civil society and representative organisations of persons with disabilities whose opinions and assistance are key for successful implementation. we will create the disability platform, which will replace the current high—level group on disability. it should support the implementation of this strategy and the national disability strategies. we will encourage member states to adopt ambitious national strategies to foster the implementation of the un convention on the rights of persons with disabilities and of the eu strategy at national, regional and local level. we will also promote the use of eu funding by member states to support the implementation of their national strategies and the un convention. only with your support and dedication can the objectives of this strategy be fully achieved. i thank you for your support, and good night.
in writing. the current covid crisis has had a huge impact on the european labour market. people with disabilities are particularly hard hit. the unemployment rate is twice as high as the population as a whole. almost a third are at risk of poverty and social exclusion. these are shocking facts and there is a need for action. we need to ensure that people with disabilities are given good opportunities in the labour market. this is the only way to ensure full participation in society. the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat discrimination. we must, as the european parliament, send a strong signal to that effect. it is up to us to put our words into action and to implement many of the tasks mentioned in the report as quickly as possible.

the next item is the report by mr josé manuel fernandes, on behalf of the committee on budgets, and mrs irene tinaglin, on behalf of the committee on economic and monetary affairs, on the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council establishing the investeu programme (com ((2020) 0403 c9-0158/2020 2020/0108 cod)) (a9-0203/2020). i have the duty to remind you that during this part-session there will be no requested speeches or blue card questions in the debate. i would also point out that, as in previous partsessions, the speakers can be reached remotely from the liaison offices of the member states.
the rapporteur. - madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank all those who worked with me on this dossier during the previous legislature. i also want to give a special hug to the rapporteur, irene tinagli, and to all the shadows from the various political groups. the european union needs public and private investment that strengthens the competitiveness of the european union, increases its productivity and, at the same time, contributes to territorial, economic and social cohesion. in this way we maintain jobs on the one hand and create quality jobs on the other. the european fund for strategic investments, known as the 'juncker plan', has mobilised over eur 500 billion and created over 1.4 million jobs after 2015. investeu should do more, not least because it brings together the 14 european financial instruments that have existed so far into a single instrument, with the aim of ensuring continuity, efficiency and synergies. it is intended to mobilise more than eur 400 billion in the period 2021-2027. in the negotiations on the multiannual financial framework in which i participated we managed - against the wishes of the council and with very little help from the european commission, to be nice - to secure an additional eur 1 billion for the investeu guarantee. we have also made important improvements in the investeu negotiations. there will be the possibility of creating sme capitalisation instruments, which is essential, which is of extreme importance and urgency in view of the consequences of the pandemic, particularly in the tourism sector and also in the catering sector. the council demonstrated that it has not learned lessons from the covid-19 pandemic crisis, as it has eliminated the window for strategic investments. however, we have managed to make these investments eligible under the four existing investeu windows. thanks to the european parliament's insistence, we have succeeded in ensuring that, on a voluntary basis, member states can use up to 5% of cohesion policy funds and up to 4% of the national envelope of the recovery and resilience facility to be able to guarantee the national investeu compartment. this national compartment is a new feature which will be an added value once again if it is used well by the member states. in the face of member states' financial difficulties, this is an excellent opportunity to create, for example, a national instrument for the solvency of small and medium-sized enterprises and national instruments for investing in sustainable infrastructure, research, smes, skills and social sphere. but this is also an opportunity that carries with it an urgency. the possibility of using this compartment should be indicated in both the national recovery plans and the partnership agreements. moreover, it makes perfect sense that investeu finances what is essential in terms of investment, but is not financed by either the national recovery plans or the partnership agreements. investeu should therefore be additional. in addition, the combination of grants and loans allows projects to be carried out that would otherwise not be possible. it is essential that overlaps are avoided and that the recovery plan and also the partnership agreements are made in a complementary way. investeu is flexible, it is open, which allows member states to be programmers of the funds rather than mere users. i also thank you, commissioner, for your commitment to the negotiations. the commission now has a responsibility to be swift in its decisions and to seek geographical balance in the use of the guarantee, even though we all know that investeu is driven by demand. small projects deserve particular attention and also permanent and coordinated work with financial intermediaries, and here it is also essential that the advisory hub, the advisory platform, works well. it is essential that it helps the territories with the greatest difficulties to attract investment, that it helps these territories to structure good applications and also helps what is an essential part of the national development banks, the national promotion banks. i also hope, finally, that the commission will help to set up cross-border investment platforms. where is the energy union, the digital union, the health union? and as this pandemic crisis has well demonstrated, we need the resilience of european industry, in particular the pharmaceutical industry. we also need autonomy, and in particular autonomy in the strategic sectors. i would like to conclude by saying that parliament has complied. it is now up to the member states to comply, the commission to be proactive and also to do its job. european citizens deserve investment and quality jobs.
rapporteur. – madam president, we are at the end of a great adventure that began in 2018 with the first commission proposal. it was a long and bumpy journey, but we can now celebrate an important success. i would like to thank the present and past components of the negotiating team in the parliament, and especially my co—rapporteur, josé manuel fernandes, for what we’ve done together. in this term we went through some exciting moments, for example when, in may 2020, the commission represented the investeu proposal with a very ambitious budget, but also through moments of great concern, for example when member states decided, during the july 2020 european council, on a very severe cut to the investeu budget line, basically making it a marginal and useless programme. we did not get discouraged. we worked to give a worthy heir to the juncker plan and also to create an ambitious programme capable of facing the challenges of our times. looking at the text of the final agreement that we will vote on today, we can say that our commitment has been rewarded. there are more funds, there are more ambitious environmental targets, and there is more national european synergy, thanks to the possibility for other implementing partners to promote projects. but our real success has been to place investeu at the heart of next generation eu. investeu is a long—term investment programme with a particular focus on smes. its four windows cover the major objectives that the union has set for the coming years: sustainability, innovation, research, social infrastructure. investeu is a programme that, perhaps more than others, highlights the paradigm shift that has taken place in the union. the real innovation of next generation eu is not just the money made available to member states. next generation eu is much more than that. it is the acknowledgement that there are individual and collective needs that are currently not fully satisfied. the financial crisis of 2008 and the economic crisis caused by the covid pandemic have shown us that there is a lack of supply of goods and services that are necessary for the well-being of citizens and firms, and bridging this gap is the challenge of our time. investeu is one of the responses that the union has put in place, but investeu is not just about resilience: it is a tool for recovery. first of all, parliament fought hard to create a synergy with the recovery and resilience facility and, thanks to our battle, member states will now be able to divert up to four percent of their funds from the recovery fund to guarantee investment projects eligible for investeu. we also managed to resurrect a fundamental tool for the recovery that the european council of july 2020 had basically eliminated: the solvency support instrument. my colleague, mr fernandes, has already mentioned that, but i would like to underline the importance of this tool. now, both the european and the national compartments of investeu will be able to provide capital support to small and medium—sized enterprises that got into trouble due to the covid-19 pandemic. it is perhaps the most important result that we, as a parliament, have achieved. but let me say that, without the strong commitment of commissioner paolo gentiloni during the negotiations, we probably would not have made it. so thank you, paolo. our work is not done yet. we still have to scrutinise the delegated acts, in particular the one on investment guidance, and we have to appoint the governance bodies, the advisory board, the steering board and the investment committee. during one of the trilogues of investeu, i realised that the majority of the people that were present in the room during the negotiations were women. investeu is therefore the result of the work and the effort of many women. so it would be nice if the commission, the eib, the implementing partners, but also the committee of the regions, the european economic and social committee and the european parliament, kept this in mind when they have to appoint their representatives in these bodies – not because it is a provision in the regulation, but because it’s the right thing to do. so thank you very much everybody.
madam chairman! we are facing a huge challenge in getting the economy back on track in the eu. the corona shutdown has left private companies with difficult opportunities to make money, and many have not had the opportunity to run their businesses at all. at the same time, we desperately need new ideas to come to life, to stimulate growth, to develop our societies and our jobs, and to become new european lighthouses. it requires taking some risks out there, but we need capital if that's going to happen, and it needs to be risk-taking, and it needs to be leveraging private investment. therefore, the timing could not be better compared to investeu. the main role of the programme is to strengthen competitiveness and growth in europe. but it's also turbocharging the green switch. it is important that the money is directed in the right direction when we are allowing public money to flow to the companies. that is why we have agreed that at least 30% of the money should be spent on climate change. i would have liked to see that percentage much higher. it would have given us better opportunities to give a hand in the back of the companies that are trying right now to create the new green solutions of the future - the solutions that we really, really need - and so it's out in the companies, in the market in the real world, that the change has to happen and the solutions have to be found. so i hope that everybody remembers that it's at least 30 percent. the percentage may well end up being much higher.
madam president, as we identified in the current sm europe survey, one of the most important prerequisites for overcoming the pandemic is improving access to capital and, above all, reducing red tape. smes have been hit hard by the crisis. half of them are facing extinction. not only would many jobs be lost, but also the unique know-how and technologies they have created. unlike jobs, these will not be replaced easily. i am very pleased to be here to talk about the investeu programme. it is an important programme that aims to help create jobs and help entrepreneurs get through this period by improving access to capital. i appreciate that it is specifically focused on the recovery of smes, small businesses, digitalisation and a sustainable economy. many were sceptical when the former president of the european commission presented the efsi plan that this programme follows, but we see that it has borne fruit and stimulated a sufficient volume of investment. many also argued that the previous programme contained a lot of bureaucracy and that this discouraged many entrepreneurs from applying. but i believe that this new investeu programme will largely do away with unnecessary red tape and will make the assessment faster so that firms can borrow as soon as possible. i want the money from investeu to reach as many smes as possible, supporting many important jobs.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, i shall speak in italian today. in italy, the economic crisis of recent decades is a crisis of investment, particularly private investment. between 2008 and 2018, italy lost 20 billion in private investment, which will decrease by another 16% in 2020, and foreign direct investment is drying up. this is why investeu is so important, as it will mobilise over 400 billion in resources. state intervention and the revival of household consumption, which is important, are not enough. private investment is the lever for wealth creation and redistribution, which is why i have to ask myself not how many bonuses i distribute with european money, but how many new jobs i create for girls and boys in the environmental and digital sectors. i don't have to ask how many sectors i'm putting a patch on or how many people i'm helping with the public aid i.v. but how i'm getting restaurants, tourism businesses, small and medium-sized businesses back on their feet. this is how you rebuild trust and hope in a country, this is how you do politics in the highest and noblest sense of the word.
in the name of the id group. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, today we are voting on an ambitious new european health programme, for which i have had the honour of being the rapporteur for the id group. the overall objective of the programme is to step up the health response through the provision of funding to member states in order to increase investment in treatment and prevention, by addressing mental illness, which is an indirect cause of sharply increasing pandemic effects, and by creating a resilient and flexible environment. although the new programme started as a dedicated and autonomous funding programme with a budget of €9.4 billion, during the negotiations on the multiannual financial framework the funds were cut drastically to a proposed €1.7 billion. the strong position of parliament has enabled the appropriation to be increased to the appropriate figure of 5.1 billion. allocating at least 20% of the budget to health prevention and promotion, advancing the digitization of health care and preparing for any future pandemics are among the main parts of the agreement, as well as the prevention of cancer and cardiovascular diseases. the shortcomings in health care in the member states are many and depend also on a lack of funds, which it is necessary and urgent to channel in an appropriate way in order to return health care to the central role it should play. in this sense, european funds represent an opportunity to be seized to strengthen our health systems and to promote the production of active pharmaceutical ingredients within the union, to minimise dependence on the production of third countries and to exploit our excellence in the field.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, europe has failed first in the health emergency linked to the pandemic and now at the vaccination stage. you talk about cooperation, coordination, prevention, rapid response, all right, all right, but the facts? israel, britain and the united states are all countries that are doing better than us. even morocco is ahead of us in terms of vaccination. every day we hear about cuts in the number of doses that manufacturers will deliver to europe. president von der leyen says that there will certainly be retaliation. unfortunately, there are no penalties for late delivery in the contracts we signed, so you don't know what retaliation i'm talking about. austria and denmark have already announced agreements with israel to research and produce vaccines, thus saying "goodbye" to the exclusive reliance on europe. the positive thing is that the european union has followed the league's positions. in italy, draghi did well to block the export of european-made vaccines to australia. it is right that europe should now block the export of vaccines to countries outside the european union in the same way. but the european medicines agency is too slow with vaccine approvals, and european citizens are beginning to think that europe will not save them, but rather that its bureaucracy and its red tape will sink them. so europe has to wake up, it has to implement the vaccine plan and think first of the european countries, as italy is doing, and then of the others.
in the name of the id group. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, one year after the covid, the situation of fisheries today remains tragic. in this context, the revision of the control regulation is both anticipated and worrying. parliament has certainly improved the proposal and i thank the rapporteur for accepting our amendments, including the derogations for small pelagic species, the use of undersized animals for charitable purposes and the quantities for direct sale. but that's not enough. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the fishing industry is not left behind. i'm reiterating our opposition to cctv. but we've been working with the industry and our government to come up with a sensible alternative. if the cameras are to be installed, only as an incidental sanction, then they should be temporary and removed after a maximum of 3-6 months. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the fishing vessels are not subject to the landing obligation. we have a great responsibility to thousands of businesses, fishermen and their families. let's not forget that when we make such important decisions. and the commission does not forget this either, especially when it uses questionable methods to influence the vote of this parliament: the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i therefore ask the commissioner to clarify.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the eu's common fisheries policy must aim to ensure the health of the marine ecosystem, and hence the sustainable exploitation of fishery resources, and also the profitability of the fisheries sector, because fishermen are an integral part of the human ecosystem which we must preserve. this regulation on the control system must ensure that fishing is tracked, especially - i hope - imported fishing, remote electronic monitoring, the ability to count all the fish taken from our seas, transparency of fishing, effective monitoring of small-scale fishing, which does not stifle it but helps it. we import 60% of the fish we eat and it is not right to impose strict rules on our fishermen and not be sure that those who sell us fish comply with these rules. we must protect our seas, our consumers and also our fishermen. if we do not protect all three equally, we risk endorsing injustice and protecting nothing and no one.

madam president, i too would like to congratulate our colleague, sylvie guillaume, who has done an excellent job and i naturally join in the congratulations and thanks to our ombudswoman. thank you so much for your work. but most of my colleagues have already spoken about this, and i would like to point out that our parliament is also trying to give you more powers, we are trying to get the council to listen to us. we want the european ombudsman to be able to carry out inquiries of his own motion to identify repeated or particularly serious cases of maladministration; we want him to have access to more documents to help him in his investigative work; we are advocating enhanced cooperation with the ombudsmen of the member states, and all this is possible if the council listens to us. i say this because the culture of good administration, transparency and good practice must take better root, and the council must be open to it. we need the european ombudsman to be independent as a basic starting point for the exercise of his functions. we have an office, i will not say halfway, but complete, with competencies, i will not say halfway, but that can be improved, in a world that needs effective and complete governance. we need it, because providing information or good administration, good governance, cannot be a discretionary grace on the part of public authorities. it is our duty and we must abandon any docility in matters of transparency and good governance: it is crucial, especially in these times of hyper-reinforced executives.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank the european ombudsman, mrs o'reilly. we 're in a tough time . the pandemic that has hit us for a year has exacerbated economic crises and social inequalities. in my country, italy, in 2020, the number of people in absolute poverty increased by more than one million compared to the previous year, and similar figures are coming in from other member states. in the light of the historical moment we are living in, your role is becoming increasingly important, as it is essential to strengthen the trust of the european citizens in the union, a trust which has been undermined too many times, as for example in the recent chaos over the purchase and supply of vaccines. mrs o'reilly, there is still a long way to go to achieve the high standards of good administration that european citizens expect, but i want to say that i appreciate your ethical approach and i appreciate the investigations you have launched into transparency, particularly on the ema, the european medicines agency. finally, let me thank you for your commitment to protecting and guaranteeing the rights of disabled people, which are too often not taken into account.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, as i do every year, i was about to congratulate the european ombudsman on her work. but this time i shall use the minute i have to denounce a problem which was too quickly mentioned in paragraph 46 of the guillaume report. the ombudsman proposed that a specific leave entitlement be granted to eu staff members who become parents as a result of surrogacy. i strongly object to this implicit approval of surrogacy. surrogacy is a barbaric practice whereby a wealthy couple rent the womb of a poor woman and take the child she has borne. it is a new form of slavery, and women are once again the victims. by endorsing it, the union betrays the values it claims to uphold. she's sacrificing women's respect on the altar of a frighteningly expanding business. i'll never accept that. if, in the end, there is to be only one true feminist in this house, it will be me.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, madam ombudsman, i welcome the high level of vigilance for maintaining high standards of transparency and ethics, even in an unexpected situation such as the health crisis. transparency remains the main complaint. recent work on the revolving door problem is important. following the investigations completed, we expect the commission to take a more rigorous approach by following up the recommendations made. the ombudsman's role in relation to disability is important: i would remind you that full and consistent implementation of the un convention on the rights of persons with disabilities is essential. finally, the ombudsman's initiative is important, as it has led the ema to introduce measures to increase transparency in areas such as clinical trials. this is all the more relevant now, in the wake of the pandemic, given the centrality of the debate on health nationalism and the demand, also from this parliament, for greater transparency on contracts with pharmaceutical companies and greater solidarity between member states.
in the name of the id group. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, looking to the future means acknowledging the mistakes that have been made in the past, and to do so requires the humility to recognise that some of the recipes adopted in the past have been counterproductive and may have brought the european union to the brink of recession: we are not talking about the future of the community, but about the future of the european union. addressing the economic impact of the pandemic means thinking about nextgenerationeu, which has indeed had too long a timetable, and so there is a fear that the stimulus to the economy is not enough, despite the mountain of debts that will weigh on future generations, despite the too many conditionalities still present in the text of the regulation, a kind of sword of damocles that will have consequences on the budget planning of states in the coming years. addressing the economic impact of the pandemic requires the ability to listen, without necessarily dividing europe between an army of uncritical yes men and those who are used to analyzing, reasoning and possibly criticizing those tools that do not work.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner gentiloni, thank you for being here. being competitive in the times of covid means accepting a new challenge in which to create efficiency and competitiveness. there are two key points that must be achieved if we are to be successful in investing: the speed of execution. the sooner we invest, the sooner the economy starts up again. this also means simplifying and bureaucratizing, with an eye to micro-enterprises in particular. and then the quality of the projects: digitalization is key, but if we don't take advantage of the change in methods and timing to unbureaucratize, to make everything simpler, to revise procedures and processes, we have failed. one last point: the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. do you think you can put some pressure on these states? in particular, for example, in the case of italy's procurement code, do you think you would advise the new president draghi to review it and align it with the simplest european procedures?
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, in italy alone, in 2020, one million more people are living in poverty. one million is not a slogan but a figure supplied by the national institute of statistics. the increase in absolute poverty is greatest in the north of our country, the north made up of small and mediumsized enterprises and vat items, which are the real productive engine of italy and europe. i would like to say to you and to commissioner gentiloni: do we want to find a way to counter the effect of the pandemic on the economy? we are abolishing red tape, unnecessary regulations and complex procedures which cost businessmen money and time and reduce the productivity and competitiveness of our enterprises. finally, to stimulate public investment, let us not make the mistake of reintroducing the stability pact: we need more flexibility, not more austerity. in short, let us leave our businesses to their own devices, let them rise up with their own strength, which is increasing, and their strength is their commitment and their capacity to produce.

mr president, ladies and gentlemen, 384 000 people have died, more than 5 million have fled abroad, 6 million are internally displaced, 90% of the population is living in extreme poverty and more than 100 000 political prisoners are being held by the assad regime. this is the terrifying balance we face, ten years after a revolt that had instead ignited hope. a tragic balance, which calls us to play a diplomatic role, of course, but also a humanitarian one, first of all towards those who are fleeing from that horror or who too often remain stranded, rejected at our borders, in our guilty immobility as european states and institutions. syrian refugees often wish they could go home, but they can't. our duty is to provide them with support, humanitarian corridors, genuine reception. and of course we must stand with those who no longer want a blood regime, an enemy of the values we believe in. finally, i believe we must tell ourselves the truth and admit our mistakes: we haven't always made the right choices in the past.
in the name of the id group. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, vice-president dombrovskis, commissioner schmit, i am surprised but also pleased that the european institutions have finally acknowledged the need to review the economic governance of the union and in particular the stability and growth pact. these issues have always been the basis of the political action of my party, the league, drawing fierce criticism to the point of ghettoization, with a pretexted and undemocratic cordon sanitaire by those who today, without coherence, share our same demands for revision. our economists, senator alberto bagnai, mr claudio borghi aquilini and the independents, professor paolo savona and president luciano barra caracciolo, have always been in favour of revising these obsolete and counterproductive rules. was a pandemic necessary to reach these conclusions, given that the same rules of the stability pact, the six pack, the two pack and the fiscal compact, in a rossinian crescendo, have even more negatively conditioned the recovery of the eurozone states after the 2008 crisis? what if there had been no monetary stimulus by the ecb, such as qe and the subsequent pepp, which drew initial criticism of draghi, but which at least partially overcame mismanagement? finally, the package we are about to vote on would only allow us to agree to the introduction of a climate indicator if green investment is excluded from the deficit calculation.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, it took a pandemic to affirm two principles dear to the five star movement: "no one saves himself" and "leave no one behind". these led to the recovery fund and the freezing of the stability pact. we believe that a third principle is now needed, namely 'europe for all'. the key to achieving this is to radically change the european semester and all existing governance mechanisms, placing environmental and social objectives on the same level as macroeconomic ones. we don't have to make up anything. it's all written. the sustainable development goals and the european pillar of social rights have been known for years, but they will move from being mere statements of principle to objectives to be pursued in practice by the member states. it will result in a different governance, based on a golden rule whereby states will no longer be admonished if they refuse to cut social spending and which instead provides for the exclusion of green and social investment from the deficit account. a gentle revolution is at hand and with it the future of europe.

in the name of the id group. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank the rapporteur, mr lópez aguilar, for the text he has proposed, which is well-articulated but unfortunately embedded in ideological positions which undermine its content. what does protecting children mean to you? one in four european children is at risk of poverty or social exclusion, not to mention children who are victims of physical or psychological violence, abuse or exploitation. children are exposed today more than ever to threats not only in the real world but also on the web, even more dangerous because they are hidden. for young people, the online space is a vital space: the city squares and streets have been replaced by virtual spaces, which it is our duty to make safe. investing in children means providing them with education, opportunities, treatment, eliminating the fragility of their disability, and certainly not changing the "migrant narrative", introducing sex education in schools or integrating the roma, who use their traditions as an alibi for the lack of schooling of children. i believe that these are not the main issues in today's debate and that their only result is to divide rather than unite, boycotting the common effort we must make to defend our children seriously and to ensure a better future for them. for these reasons, we will abstain.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i am very concerned about the situation of children in europe. the pandemic and lockdown have led to an increase in incidents of psychological distress, depression, and domestic violence. in italy alone, an estimated 20% increase in suicide attempts among younger people: between closed schools and endless hours in front of screens, too many of them have fallen into extreme forms of self-harm and deadly social media challenges. i want to remind you of the recent case of antonella sicomoro, an italian girl who was the victim, at just 10 years old, of a crazy game on tiktok. if we're going to really get back on track, kids need to take back their sociability. serious measures must be taken to get them back to school and to focus on their training so that they can develop the skills they need for their future. although they are less affected by the virus, they are the ones who are likely to pay the highest price. what will happen to generations in the midst of a pandemic? we can't leave them alone and we have to think about their families.
in the name of the id group. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, the openlux investigation and the tax justice network report once again highlight the inadequacy of the european union's taxation system in combating tax evasion and fraud. the multinationals, thanks also to the recent restrictions on mobility, continue to profit from the culpable inertia of governments and european institutions, accumulating profits without fulfilling the redistributive obligations which our legal systems place on all economic actors. some countries practice tax dumping to the detriment of others, by taking revenue away from them and encouraging the evasive behaviour of companies which, thanks to the single market, relocate their tax headquarters where it is most convenient. this draining of billions of euros from the public coffers of the states forces small businesses and households to pay more taxes with greater sacrifices. the portuguese presidency is working to achieve public reporting which would require multinational companies to declare the profits made in each country in which they operate. this would not only highlight the shifting of profits and related revenue, but would discourage it. but the obstructionism of some countries has so far blocked everything. today we must show our citizens that the european parliament is here to protect them. private companies pursue their own profits, institutions must look after the public interest. so i ask all of you, ladies and gentlemen, to proceed with courage in this work, resisting the undue pressure that the lobbies will exert to preserve their privileges.
author. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, thank you for your sympathy and condolences on the loss of our compatriots, ambassador luca attanasio and carabiniere vittorio iacovacci, who were brutally murdered along with their driver three weeks ago in the democratic republic of congo. almost a month after this vile attack, the investigation has still not led to the identification of the killers. among the many suspects are local brigands rather than jihadist groups affiliated since 2018 with isis. the gravity of the facts, as well as the persistence of this violence in an area particularly rich in mineral resources, requires europe to pay no more attention to the region but to take an active part in it, starting with the investigations which the un is carrying out and on which i believe europe should join italy in demanding clarifications. the monusco peacekeeping mission employs 14 000 soldiers, has been operating for more than 20 years and costs almost one billion euros a year: how is it that with so many resources deployed, our men have not been protected? that is why, until the full light is shed on this tragedy, we must consider suspending cooperation with the congolese authorities and european programmes co-financed by the commission and parliament. the attack on 22 february is all the more serious because it comes just a few months after another violent attack on an italian diplomat, the consul in gedda, stefano stucci, a sign that the profession of the diplomat has become more dangerous, especially in areas of the world where economic and political interests are intertwined with guerrilla warfare and instability. we must also do more to defend our diplomatic representations: it is our job to bring justice to the victims and to hold them accountable. we owe it to the families, we owe it to pay proper tribute to those who lost their lives serving their country far from home and we owe it to the fellow soldiers of the carabinieri, always in the front line for the peace and security of all of us.

madam president, ladies and gentlemen, let us first of all pay tribute to the italian ambassador luca attanasio and the two other victims, whose murder is, unfortunately, a reminder of the risks faced by humanitarian personnel. the case concerned a world food programme convoy in eastern congo, which is struggling with the proliferation of armed groups. in 2019, there were more than 130 in south and north kivu alone, compared to 70 in 2015. the key is some 1.8 million people displaced in 2018, a record after ethiopia, and 12.8 million reduced to poverty. in the face of this record, the denunciation of the protagonists remains elusive, whether it is the shadowy role played for a time by rwanda or the links suspected by the un mission itself between the ugandan armed group of the allied democratic forces and the international islamist terrorism that, from al-shabab to al qaeda, ravages the african continent. our lucid analysis of the causes of this conflict will determine the eventual resolution of africa's longest-running humanitarian crisis.

madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the congo is rich, it has everything: gold, diamonds and coltan, the new black gold that goes into our smartphones, computers and batteries. but congo is also home to 120 armed groups, fueling a war that has killed tens of thousands of civilians since 2017. more than five million people have been forced to flee their homes in the mineral-rich areas where luca attanasio, vittorio iacovacci and mustafa milambo were brutally murdered. in the congo, more than 22 million people are hungry and five million children are malnourished. i have quoted many numbers, because numbers speak the language of truth. the truth is that congo is raped for its riches; the truth is that exports fuel wars that starve and kill; the truth is that europe, with its market of 500 million consumers, has a duty to change things. the truth is that by imposing import conditions, we are saying who we are and promoting our values. to continue as if nothing is happening in the name of profit makes us accomplices.

in writing. when we talk about vision, we must first see the problems, the needs of people living in the regions. obviously, there are some aspects that are not being explored at the moment, and that's why we're seeing a declining population in the regions, with more and more farms being pulled in. the people of the region need jobs and an income that will enable them to live with dignity. then no one will have to seek a better life in the cities or seek happiness in other lands. people need basic services that are easily accessible. we need infrastructure to enable access to those services. by disconnecting the urban and rural, by optimising, we are condemning the regions. and this is not just done at the regional level, but at the national level. inequalities in direct payments condemn some farmers to poverty and create exclusion for others. attempts to reduce funding for cohesion is the desire to continue to see a widening gap and the backwardness of certain regions. so, to make real change, we need to have the courage to see the problems and to tackle them, creating a more solidarity-based, more equal europe.
in writing. the announcement by the european commission of a communication on the long-term vision for europe's rural areas has been well received, as it stimulates debate on the future of territories that not only provide food, but are the most representative of our identity, need their own and independent infrastructure, and whose inhabitants deserve the same standard of living as those in urban areas. the spanish socialist delegation is calling for a genuine rural agenda with a comprehensive vision for the development of our rural areas. this approach is not limited to agriculture, tourism or the environment, but is transversal to all the basic needs of these areas. this will not be possible unless we succeed in involving local actors - municipalities and inhabitants of these territories - in the design of this long-term vision. the report also makes special mention of rural women, who must be present and visible in the preparation of the communication. we also believe that the conference on the future of europe presents a great opportunity to develop the debate on the future of our rural areas.
the next item is the debate on the request for an oral answer to the commission on the evaluation of the geoblocking regulation by anna cavazzini, on behalf of the committee on the internal market and consumer protection (o-000010/2021 b9-0008/21) (2021/2546 ((rsp)).
member of the commission. – mr president, honourable members, i would like to thank the committee on the internal market and consumer protection (imco) for this question. in line with the conclusions of the first report of the geo—blocking regulation, i am happy to provide the following additional elements following the structure of the parliament’s questions. with regard to enforcement, the commission fully shares the parliament’s view on the importance of ensuring the effective enforcement of the current geo—blocking rules. for this reason, the commission is also fully engaged in cooperating with the competent national authorities to ensure appropriate enforcement of the regulation, including within the consumer protection cooperation (cpc) network. in this regard, it plans, first of all, to steer and monitor the cooperation as regards geo—blocking—related aspects of the coordinated enforcement activities of the cpc network. this adds to ad hoc discussions within the cpc network and guidance on issues raised as regards the coordinated enforcement and investigation cases. moreover, in order to facilitate enforcement – but also compliance – the commission will provide additional guidance within the context of the planned update of the service directive handbook, as well as in updating the clarifications on specific implementation questions on the basis of the issues discussed within the cpc network. with regard to the cross—border delivery, as mentioned in the parliamentary question, it remains a business choice of the trader to provide cross—border delivery or not. nevertheless, artificial segmentation of markets by traders preventing access without any objective justification should be avoided. the commission would like to stress that the geo—blocking regulation is a part of a wider range of eu measures to facilitate the active engagement of traders cross—border. moreover, the pandemic has triggered significant changes in the evolution of e—commerce. the commission will therefore closely monitor the implementation and first effects of such complementary measures, as well as the evolution of cross—border e—commerce, and report its findings in the context of another stocktaking exercise in the regulation in 2022. with regard to the audio—visual content, the commission will engage with stakeholders in a dialogue to identify how to foster better circulation of such content across the union. as indicated in the recent media and audio—visual action plan, the objective of the dialogue is to identify concrete steps to improve the access to and availability of audio—visual content across borders in the union. the dialogue will gather representatives from the audio—visual industry, consumer organisations and other interested parties, such as national film funds. it will be launched in the first half of this year. in practice, the dialogue could help define specific targets to be reached to improve the access to and availability of audio—visual works across the union and to identify concrete actions that could be taken to achieve those targets. the commission expects the industry producers, distributors, broadcasters, online services and other interested parties to engage constructively in the dialogue and to commit to take action. the commission will monitor the progress in the achievement of the specific targets in cooperation with the audio—visual sector. the commission will look in particular at any improvements achieved in terms of wider circulation of audio—visual works and consumers’ access to a variety of audio—visual content. the commission will take stock of the progress achieved by the end of 2022 and decide on the follow—up, assessing various options, including legislative interventions.
on behalf of the ppe group. – mr president, i would like to thank the commissioner very much. yes, geo-blocking: the situation is improving but it’s still a very serious problem for many europeans, and as anna mentioned, especially the digital single market needs improvement. i will give you a concrete example. recently, i wanted to see a film that was available on a portuguese video-on-demand platform financed from the eu funds. but i was not able to watch it because i was geo-blocked. the number of those who want to access digital content cross-border grows very rapidly, and half of them fail. so it means that millions of citizens in the european union are hindered from benefits which the digital single market should bring them. and what about those who belong to linguistic minorities and who are deprived of access to culture in their own language? we like to paraphrase the famous sentence of massimo d’azeglio and use it in the european context: we made a european union; now we need europeans. commissioner, this goal will not be achieved in a union that passively watches how the freedom of media is being destroyed in some of our member states, how geo-blocking divides us along the internal borders of the european union. and the commission talks very beautifully. it’s time to act, commissioner. we impatiently expect positive, tangible, concrete results.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the extension of geoblocking is right, but if the scope of this regulation were extended to audiovisual services, it would be much more difficult to continue to finance the creation of these services and content, with serious consequences for employment in this sector, which is so important in many european countries. if the principle of territoriality were to be abandoned, we would have a greater concentration of supply in the hands of a few dominant players, to the detriment of alternative and independent supply and to the detriment of pluralism and cultural diversity. the commission's evaluation report notes that this would not bring any benefits to consumers, who already benefit from cross-border access to cultural content thanks to the portability regulation, which does not call into question the principle of territoriality. we are instead expanding the supply of crossborder cultural content and strengthening the dissemination of works and co-productions with the media programme. the most serious problem today is the increased use of large online platforms in the face of cinema and cultural space closures, which are putting a strain on authors, businesses and the work of the entire sector.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, minister, i am witnessing in my country a debate which is serious, important and dramatic but also a little surreal. the commission's mistakes are being referred to as 'well, it has little competence, we need more europe'. on the mistakes made - because they were made - in the regions of my country, where my party is also in government, they say "no, they have too many skills, we have to take them away". i think we all need to be a little more balanced and those who make mistakes have to pay. i inform you, and i think you know this, that those who have done wrong in my country are paying the price, both technically and politically. i would also like to see someone in the european commission pay up or resign, because if we are complaining about these contracts, which we have all been calling for more transparency on from the start and which we have only just been able to see, then someone has to give us answers. all right, attack the big pharma and all the manufacturers, all right, i'm all for it, but let's find a solution because out here people, who are worried, don't care whether i'm in the majority or the opposition: they want answers from all of us together, possibly perhaps in the style of the current italian government, which talks less and tries to do a little more.

madam president, i have three questions. the first concerns the health implications. although i support the idea of coordination at european level to guarantee free movement, i think that this certificate gives the false impression that everything is settled. we still do not know whether authorised vaccines prevent people from transmitting the virus, nor how long they last. who, for example, has raised these concerns. so why not consider these important medical considerations? secondly, you mention the possibility for member states not to recognise the certificate in the same way. do you not think that more coordination would be necessary before we put in place a system which might lead to discrimination? thirdly, with regard to personal data, considering the medical content of the information, why not opt for a system that simply mentions whether a test, a vaccine or a cure has taken place in order to avoid the possible tracking of citizens' health data?
