on behalf of the verts/ale group. – madam president, memorial was founded and began to thrive in the waning days of the soviet union. they have valiantly followed their founding spirit with their anti—stalinist work until this day. all these years, they have been upholding the values that we all hold dear. in 2009, the european parliament honoured them by bestowing upon them the prize that carries the name of their co—founder, sakharov. now, vladimir putin, a late admirer and disciple of stalin, wants to close down memorial – to ‘liquidate’ memorial, as they say. even his language gives testimony to what he represents. we today express our unbreakable solidarity with memorial. the torch with which the courageous people of memorial have carried over 30 years will be carried on. memorial represents the dignity and europeanness of russia.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, we have been debating russia, president putin, and his methods of suppressing human rights, freedom of speech and democracy virtually every day this week. memorial is a legend among human rights organizations, and the putin regime has long sought to eliminate it. this organization is inconvenient for putin and probably dangerous. it maps past and present political crimes committed against the people of russia and other nations. he's not a foreign agent. it's not an extremist organization. memorial is helping foreign researchers search for clues in the archives. for the last five years since 2016 memorial has also had a czech branch. the memorial has also helped to map the fate of some of the more than a thousand czechs who were executed in the former soviet union. fortunately, the russian prosecutor's office cannot threaten the czech branch, but our challenge comes at a time when the russian prosecutor's office is once again taking action against memorial in russia. the european union and the member states should impose and support sanctions against all russian officials who unlawfully impede the work and very existence of memorial and similar organisations. member states should do their utmost to preserve valuable historical archives, memories and support the memorial branches in their territories. thank you to everyone who supported this challenge.
madam president, the attempts to intimidate, silence and, ultimately, close memorial are not only an example of how far putin is prepared to go, they are an insult to the russian people, to their history. we have a problem with the putin kleptocrat autocracy, not with the russian people. if we let them down, those who fight for the values we hold sacred, we also allow the undermining of these same values. what do sakharov and memorial teach us? we are reminded of soviet-era intimidation, oppression and atrocities, and we get confronted with the inspiration they still provide to us today. arguably, the most important instrument we have is to raise our voice and keep telling their stories, the stories of the countless killed in russia and abroad, those prosecuted and imprisoned, people who simply stood up for their rights, for the environment, for political freedoms or stood as an opposition candidate. let me share with you a lesser—known contemporary story to pay tribute to all of them. on 15 july 2021, andrei borovikov was convicted to 27 months in prison by the appeals court of arkhangelsk. allegedly, he spread pornography by sharing a generally available music clip on his profile on the russian alternative to facebook. this, of course, was one of these commonly fabricated charges to be able to convict him. who was borovikov? who is borovikov? he was allied as a local coordinator to aleksei navalny. he has a wife and a newborn son. he’s now detained a thousand kilometres from his home, on purpose, moved there after being convicted to make it very difficult for his family to visit him – to break him. keep telling their stories, past and present.
mr. president, the attempt to shut down russia's most respected human rights organization, memorial, is also an attempt to deny the memory of the russian nation. in many cases, the fsb is assisted by the so-called economic and social committee of experts. it is this pseudoscientific body that has drafted the statement calling for the closure of memorial. the same quasi-scientific body also acted as an expert in the so-called sandarmoh case, when the head of the republic of karelia memorial, yuri dmitriev, was sentenced to a severe prison sentence on trumped-up charges. he had found and brought out victims of stalin's persecution from under the pine forests of karelia. the third case is from the kuolan peninsula. agnes haikara, a descendant of finnish immigrants, wrote a book about the tragic fate of norwegian and finnish immigrants during stalin's persecution. a year ago, the fsb confiscated the book based on the opinion of this pseudo-scientific committee. today the european parliament is calling for sanctions to be imposed on those who have been involved in the closure of memorial. ladies and gentlemen, the committee of economic and social experts is a suitable target for sanctions.
i'm not going to let you get away with this. ladies and gentlemen! i'm one of those - and there aren't that many of us in this room - who remember the sakharov prize being awarded to the sakharov memorial twelve years ago. we remember the visit of mrs. alexeyeva, mr. kowaliev, this other face of russia, and also their sometimes bitter words directed at the west. but i think what's happening now with memorial is a regression, because this organization could have been functioning a couple of years ago, it could have been functioning a few years ago. she was marginalized, she was pushed, the repression that we have today was not there then. i think that also shows maybe the fear of the russian power elite, but it's also a signal of the need for our solidarity.
i'm not going to let you get away with this. ladies and gentlemen! history is not only the perfect teacher of life, but also the effective conscience of nations. the attempt to destroy the memorial is nothing more than an attempt to silence the conscience, which reminds the russian authorities of the crimes of the communist system. the soviet union was a barbaric state built on terror, forced labor and mass deportations. they affected many ethnic and social groups who did not want to accept soviet slavery. however, the first victims of the loggers and the shippers were the russians themselves. it was for them that the memorial was created, to show the truth about communism to those who would never have heard about it in school or in the media. you can't build a partnership with russia, where the authorities are still proud of the evil that the soviet union did to the eastern bloc countries. the memorial has been defending the right to the truth since 1989. we're supposed to be protecting the memorial.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, we have said this many times in this house and i think it is right to say it again: vladimir putin's biggest problem is the strength of a free, informed, active civil society. its real enemy is democracy in all its forms and expressions, inside or outside russia's borders. now putin's targets include the international memorial society, not only a symbolic organization for russian civil society, but also a concrete reference point in the struggle for human rights and the active defense of human rights. the demands for the dissolution of memorial are clearly insane and driven by the need to silence a free and uncomfortable voice. the very law on foreign agents, incidentally, which memorial is accused of violating, is an attack on freedom of association, expression and participation. and today we stand here with memorial and all of russia's free and democratic civil society. they deserve our solidarity, our support, because only a free civil society can open up a different perspective. and in conclusion, let me say again from here that we cannot all speak about the damage caused by putin in the same way. we have his friends in this chamber, who have played with putin over the years precisely to attack our common home. i think we should not forget that.

"this message was sent to me by a citizen who lives in italy and is worried about her family. the author of all this is the communism which many in this chamber know well, either because they have experienced it or because it represents for many an uncomfortable past, a toxic love affair, difficult to recognize and justify, dotted with firing squads, torture, abuse and ill-treatment. communism, fuelled by hatred and contempt, invaded and slaughtered europe in the last century and continues to repress and suffocate, including through the so-called battalions of order, the cuban people today. to call the communist regime the republic of cuba is a contradiction in terms. a republic presupposes free elections, whereas since 1959 fake elections have been held in cuba, in which no other parties are allowed, as in the last elections held in 2018, in which all 605 elected deputies obviously belonged to the communist party of cuba. cuba is protesting against hunger and food shortages while the people are thirsty for freedom of elections and rights. ladies and gentlemen, we cannot afford to turn a blind eye to the biggest demonstration on the island since 1994. justice be done, all the instruments at our disposal be used, including the human rights clause which allows the partnership and cooperation agreement to be suspended, increasing international pressure on the ruthless and bloodthirsty regime. long live the free cuba!
in the name of the id group. mr president, thank you commissioner valean for being here, thank you also to my colleagues for being here on a subject which is very sensitive and important, despite the fact that the committee on the internal market did not have the courage to leave the direct expression of why we are here today in the text we have approved, we had to remove the references to the brenner and austrian measures. i have many austrian friends, i have a historical ally for my party in my political group, but the european commission has to intervene here, all the elements are there. i would add that the italian government is also a little too timid and should be a little more protective of its businesses, because, as commissioner valean rightly said, there is a question of proportionality and non-discrimination in the measures that have to be put in place. we have an alternative infrastructure project which is not yet many years away from being completed, but we cannot afford to penalize trade between italy and germany and not only that, other countries are involved, among productive realities which are not only destination markets, but which are also integrated markets, because together they make the added value of producing in europe positive. the problem is that if we do not find a solution because of the timidity of the italian government or because of the inaction of the european commission, the court of justice will, as it has done in the past, tell you that these measures are illegal and i would therefore advise the commission to initiate an infringement procedure, which seems to me more than legitimate. austria will give its reasons and we shall see. that is why it seems to me quite hypocritical to see a european commission which is very active, for example, on the bolkestein with regard to italy and yet is helpless in an even more serious situation such as the one we are discussing today.
